## 📱 SOCIAL CONTENT — KORAKOT'S STANDING RULES (given to Mint, 2026-07-20 · keep visible for everyone)

> These are the taste + accuracy rules Korakot taught during the Website/Social card work. They apply to **every social post & card** we make. New rules get appended here AND to `MINT.md` so they carry into every session. (Full blow-by-blow lives in the **Mint** row under Active Work.)

**Design of the image**
1. **Every card carries an IMAGE — never a text-only slide.** The image can be a real photograph, a live screenshot, **OR AI-generated art** (see 2).
2. **AI-generated images ARE allowed** for our own / generic mood shots (that's what this card used). Use **candid-style prompts** (muted colours, mild grain, imperfect framing) so it reads real, not glossy-AI. But AI is **decorative / generic ONLY**: a specific **client's actual dishes, premises or staff must be REAL (enhanced) photos — never AI-faked.** Prefer a fresh live screenshot over a reused archive.
3. Show the customer seeing the restaurant **OUTSIDE the venue** (at home / on the go). Reaching people **before** they're in the room is the whole point of website + social.

**Message & accuracy**
4. **Social media = being SEEN / staying visible** ("customers see you exist and you're active"). It is **NOT a booking channel.** Booking + online ordering belong to the **WEBSITE**. Never put a "Book now" button on a social post/mockup, and never claim posts take bookings.
5. Keep the two services distinct — **Website £5/mo** (own site, booking + ordering, 0% commission) · **Social £39/mo** (Facebook + Instagram, presence, you approve every post). Bundle = website **free** when taken with social; the one-off build is **never implied free**. Verify every price against the canonical rate card before quoting.

**Contact / CTA**
6. **Lead the CTA with the ONE-TAP channel** — Messenger (when the post is on Facebook) or **WhatsApp 07896 036386** — NOT "go to our website." "chat → website → find a form" is too many steps. Website is **"more details"** only.

**Video / reels**
7. **Reel & video ads are PORTRAIT — 1080×1920 (9:16), vertical only** (Reels / TikTok / Stories / feed). Never landscape or square for a reel. The reel factory (`reel-factory/make-reel.py`) already outputs this — keep it that way.

**Hashtags**
8. **Every post caption ends with HASHTAGS** (Korakot 2026-07-23) — relevant to the topic AND our product; ~5–8 tags, mix Thai + English + brand + topic (not 20+ spam); always include **#SiamEPOS** + an audience tag. Full hashtag bank lives in `MINT.md`.

Source: `~/Documents/SiamEPOS-Docs/marketing/cards/website-social-card.html` (+ `hero-social.html` for the FB-post mockup).

## 📜 KORAKOT'S RULES — PRODUCT & ENGINEERING (rolling · every agent reads this · append when Korakot gives you a rule in-session)

> Same idea as Mint's social rules above: when Korakot teaches a rule during a session, it gets appended HERE (and to your agent .md) so it survives into everyone's next session. Latest batch given to Krit overnight 20–21 Jul during the Twilio + AI-concierge work.

1. **Right lane, right stack.** A client's features are built on THEIR product's stack. Jinta is a spa client → the AI concierge lives in `siamepos-spa` (spa-api + spa Admin), grounded in the spa's real tables — not bolted onto the restaurant cloud because it's convenient. (Krit built the first demo on the restaurant cloud; Korakot corrected it; rebuilt properly the same night.)
2. **AI reads real data — it never invents.** Availability, prices, rota, menus come from live tables injected/tooled server-side. If the bot can't look something up, give it a tool (e.g. customers asked "who's working tomorrow?" → `list_therapists` reading the real rota) — don't let it guess.
3. **Owner-facing surfaces live in Admin, mobile-first.** No "secret link" pages — owners check things on their phones between clients, so it must be in the app they already use, easy in and out. (The chat inbox started as a keyed URL — "not practical at all" — and became Admin → AI Chats.)
4. **Escalation keeps the customer in the conversation.** When something is beyond the AI: collect the details and say a person will reply RIGHT HERE shortly. Never bounce a customer to a different phone number — only give the phone number if they explicitly ask for it.
5. **Payment is always a LINK, never in-chat.** The bot sends a booking/payment link (Stripe); card details never pass through a conversation. Deposit safety stands (Sam's W3): deposit due + no Stripe configured = refuse to silently confirm a free booking — roll back + human handoff. (Demo spa tenant currently runs **No deposit** by Korakot's explicit choice, 20 Jul.)
6. **Tokens: never accept short default expiries.** The Netlify PAT 7-day default nearly killed every site deploy. CI/service tokens get long or no expiry and live in encrypted stores (GitHub secrets / `.infra-keys`). And secrets stay Korakot-side: agents generate + verify, Korakot pastes — agents never type keys into fields.

## 🎨 KORAKOT'S RULES — CLIENT WEBSITES & MARKETING DESIGN (given to Maya, 2026-07-19/20 · rolling · every agent reads · append when Korakot gives a rule)

> Same idea as the Social + Product/Eng rule blocks above: rules Korakot taught me this session, kept here AND in `MAYA.md` so they carry into every session. Blow-by-blow lives in the **Maya** row under Active Work. Apply to **every prospect site, mockup and marketing asset** (Maya + Sandy; Mint for the card overlap).

1. **A dropped URL = run the WHOLE prospect pipeline, no asking.** Extract → archive → **build a NEW site that clearly beats theirs** → SEO analysis (`.md`) → **branded PDF**. It's an approved standing pattern — don't pause to ask "should I?", and don't stop at 4 of 5 steps (I forgot the PDF once; Korakot had to ask). One folder per client: `~/Documents/SiamEPOS-Docs/client-sites/<slug>/`.
2. **The build must hit our design standard — cinematic, NOT templated.** Korakot rejected the first Nua hero as "below your standard": eyebrow-pill + serif headline + framed photo on flat dark is the default AI look. Go **full-bleed**, lead with motion, use the client's best photography big. (A full-bleed cinematic hero fixed it.)
3. **Use the client's REAL photos — as many as are good, and build a hero VIDEO.** Don't ship 6 shots when 22 are usable; build a gallery. If the client has no video, make one from **their own stills** (Ken Burns + crossfade via ffmpeg — recipe in `client-sites/nua/make-hero-video.sh`). **Never stock footage** for a client's food/premises.
4. **Don't cold-send an "audit."** The findings (broken booking, spam pages, typos) earn the meeting **in conversation** — mailing an unsolicited fault list mis-frames us as a web agency and can insult the owner. Keep the report as our sales intel + a leave-behind once they're already talking.
5. **Always put our REAL logo on a marketing asset** — the lotus badge + "Siam EPOS" wordmark (`client/Website/logo-512.png` / `SiamEPOS-Full-Logo.*`). Never a text-only stand-in.
6. **Don't quote prices that vary.** Cheap-PC / thermal-printer prices move by supplier — keep hardware **generic** ("an ordinary PC", "a standard thermal printer"). Only fixed numbers (our subscription; a generic competitor category) belong on a card.
7. **No free trial — lead with Founder's Rate £59/mo** (Professional is normally £89). Discontinued 2026-07-17; sweep it out of every playbook/template (watch for HTML-entity-encoded Thai a plain-text grep misses).
8. **Never name a competitor with a specific %/£ claim** (ASA). Generic categories only ("the delivery apps", "£1,000+ proprietary tills"). Learned the hard way on the FB-ads incident.
9. **Website Service = £5/mo** (site + hosting + client-owned domain); the build is quoted case by case — the £199 figure is OFF the public site.
10. **Nothing reaches a client or gets posted without Korakot's approval.**

**This session's deliverables (Maya, 2026-07-20):**
- **`MAYA.md` swept:** discontinued free trial removed (9 places), named-competitor claims removed, and the prospect-pipeline rule written in as standing guidance.
- **NUA-WEB-001 DELIVERED + LIVE → https://nua-sandy.netlify.app.** Full pipeline for `eatnua.com` (4-branch Thai group: Fitzrovia · High Wycombe · Reading · Kingston). New **9-page cinematic site**: full-bleed **hero video built from their own stills**, **22 photos + gallery**, **116 dishes** rebuilt + de-duped from their WooCommerce API, per-branch `Restaurant` JSON-LD, **live booking wired to our demo cloud** (per-branch pages to dodge the widget-singleton bug), `noindex` + `robots: Disallow` + a demo-booking notice (bookings are real). **SEO report `.md` + branded `.pdf`.** Findings surfaced: **casino/SEO spam on their `/store-listing`**, truncated Fitzrovia phone, postcode typo that's propagated to their OpenTable listing, a `/watford` clone page, an indexed-but-404 story page, OCR-corrupted menu copy, and **47 cross-branch price variances**. ## 📣 MESSAGE FOR MAYA & SANDY — Key positioning insight (Nick, 2026-06-18)

**The ERP framing — use this in all marketing and design narrative.**

SiamEPOS is not just a POS system. Look at what it actually covers:

- **SiamEPOS** — sales, orders, kitchen, tables
- **SiamSpa** — bookings, staff, treatments
- **SiamShop** — product catalogue, orders, stock
- **SiamPay** — payments across all three (Phase 2)
- **Inventory** — ingredients, recipes, supplier invoices, stock levels
- **CRM** — customer database, campaigns, loyalty
- **Staff** — clock in/out, performance, wages
- **Reports** — VAT, Z reports, sales vs cost analysis
- **Website Service** *(live, 2026-07)* — we host + look after the client's website, wired to their till (live menu, booking, 0%-commission takeaway)
- **Social Media Service** *(new, 2026-07 — Mint's lane)* — we run the client's Facebook + Instagram, posts generated from their real EPOS data; pricing with Nick

That is an **ERP** — a fully integrated business operating system.
SAP and Oracle charge £50,000+ to implement and need a consultant.
SiamEPOS costs £89/month and sets up in an afternoon.

**The line to use:**
> *"The ERP built for Thai businesses in the UK — at SaaS prices."*

**Maya** — this is your positioning for Facebook groups, LINE, and any outreach. Lead with the breadth. Don't just say "POS system." Say "one system that runs your whole restaurant — till, kitchen, bookings, stock, staff, and CRM."

**Sandy** — the website and all design should reinforce this. The product should feel like one cohesive platform, not a collection of separate tools bolted together. Every screen, every feature page should feel like part of one integrated whole.

## 🎯 PHASE 1 MANDATE — ALL AGENTS (2026-06-16)

**Korakot's direction: Phase 1 only. One goal across the whole team — get paying clients.**

Three products. All three need their first client live before anything else matters.

| Product | First target | Agent responsible |
|---------|-------------|-------------------|
| **SiamEPOS** (restaurant POS) | First Thai restaurant signed + live | Krit, Sandy, Maya, Nook |
| **SiamSpa** (spa management) | First Thai spa signed + live | Sam, Sandy, Maya |
| **SiamShop** (Thai supermarket e-commerce) | Demo-ready → first supermarket live | Joy, Sandy |

**Rules for every agent while Phase 1 is active:**
- ❌ No new feature work unless it directly closes a sale or unblocks a client going live
- ❌ Do not start Phase 2 (SiamPay) or Phase 3 (SiamTransfer) work — those are parked
- ✅ Stability, polish, demos, onboarding, and outreach only
- ✅ If a prospect asks for a feature, log it — don't build it yet

**What each agent is doing in Phase 1:**
- **Krit** — Keep SiamEPOS stable. Fix bugs immediately. Support client onboarding. No new tickets.
- **Sandy** — siamepos.co.uk must convert visitors. Demo sites (siamepos.net, spa demo) must look sharp for sales calls.
- **Maya** — Outreach to Thai community: restaurants, spas, supermarkets. Facebook groups, LINE, WhatsApp. Every week push for leads.
- **Nick** — Advise on pricing, pitch, and deals. Help close. No new strategic planning until first client is signed.
- **Nook** — QA every product before client demos. Nothing goes in front of a prospect that has a visible bug.
- **Kai** — Inventory module demo-ready. Support Krit on any client onboarding questions.
- **Sam** — SiamSpa demo-ready and stable for first prospect call.
- **Joy** — SIAMSHOP-001 → 005 (scaffold → catalogue → checkout → emails → order admin). First demo = first client opportunity.
- **Pose** — Subscription billing working cleanly for new signups on all three products.

Phase 2 and Phase 3 will begin only when at least one client is live on each Phase 1 product.

## 🟢 RESOLVED on the demo spa — `JWT_SECRET` set on SiamSpa Railway (Krit + Korakot, 2026-06-23)

> ✅ **DONE on `spa-api.siamepos.co.uk`** — Korakot set a strong `JWT_SECRET` via the Railway dashboard; verified live (set-credentials accepts the new secret → 400 on empty body, rejects the public default → 403). Owner-login provisioning from the back-office now works against this spa. **Pose: still required on every NEW spa/SiamShop deploy + the restaurant tenants' `AUTH_SECRET` — keep this on the new-deploy env checklist.**

**Was a live, exploitable hole on the spa cloud — closed in code, then ops finished it.**

While stress-testing the spa I found `spa-api.siamepos.co.uk` had **no `JWT_SECRET` set** (and no `NODE_ENV=production`). The repo's public default secret `dev-only-change-me` was therefore in force, so **anyone could forge an admin JWT** and call protected endpoints — and `POST /api/auth/set-credentials` (gated by that same secret) could be used to **mint an admin owner login**. I confirmed it live (created + then deactivated a throwaway admin id 8), then closed it.

**Code fix (shipped, live on spa):** `JWT_SECRET` **and** `SETUP_SECRET` now fall back to a **random per-boot secret** whenever unset or equal to the public default — no `NODE_ENV` dependency (the restaurant's SEPOS-061 only checked production; the spa Railway doesn't set `NODE_ENV`, which is exactly why the mitigation didn't fire). Files: `siamepos-spa/src/middleware/auth.js`, `siamepos-spa/src/routes/auth.js`. The hole is closed regardless of ops action.

**Why ops STILL must set it (now required, not optional):**
1. With a random per-boot secret, **every owner/staff session resets on each Railway restart/redeploy** — annoying for owners signing in on mobile.
2. **Owner remote login can't be provisioned until it's set.** The new back-office "Provision owner login" button (below) POSTs the tenant's `/api/auth/set-credentials` with `X-Setup-Secret` = the tenant's `JWT_SECRET`. Until a real secret is set on the spa Railway, that call (correctly) returns **403** and no owner login can be created.

**Do this per spa deploy:** set a long random `JWT_SECRET` on the spa's Railway service (same as the restaurant's `AUTH_SECRET` story — see the open Korakot manual-action above). Then paste that same value into the back-office once via the new owner-login provisioning field; it's remembered in client metadata after. Same applies to the restaurant tenants' `AUTH_SECRET` (already flagged) and any new SiamShop deploys.

## 🌐 DOMAIN BUNDLING — decided (Korakot + Nick, 2026-07-17)

Going with **domain bundling, NOT a Namecheap affiliate program.** SiamEPOS registers + manages each client's domain and **includes it in the £5/mo Website Service** — pitch: *"website + hosting + domain, all managed, £5/month."* Adds recurring margin + real lock-in (domain → Cloudflare DNS → Pages → site → EPOS) and fixes the apex-SSL gotcha because we own the nameservers.
- **Guardrail:** register in the **CLIENT's name — they OWN the domain**, SiamEPOS is admin/manager only. Never hold a client's domain hostage; it must be portable if they leave (trust, not force).
- **✅ Krit (ops) DONE 2026-07-17:** (a) Reseller-account reality check: Namecheap discontinued the classic reseller program — our EXISTING API account is the vehicle (retail ~£7-9/yr fits fine inside £60/yr service revenue; ResellerClub only worth it at 50+ domains). (b) **Expiry + auto-renew tracking LIVE:** Control Room → Domains tab now pulls the FULL Namecheap account via the API (new client domains appear automatically) with per-domain auto-renew chips — red "auto-renew OFF ⚠️" warning surfaces lapse risk; whois fallback if the API/IP-whitelist is down. First pull found **all 7 domains auto-renew ON** (and discovered siammart.co.uk the old hardcoded list missed). ⚠️ Recurring gotcha: Korakot's home IP rotates — each rotation needs the new IP added to Namecheap's API whitelist (Profile → Tools → API Access); Domains tab falls back to whois gracefully meanwhile. (c) Registering client domains in the CLIENT's name = standing practice from the next website client.

## 📣 NEW SERVICE FOR THE WHOLE TEAM — SiamEPOS now HOSTS client websites (Korakot via Krit, 2026-07-15)

**We can now host client restaurant websites on our own infrastructure at ~£0 cost** (free GitHub Actions → Netlify pipeline, same stack the app/POS sites run on). This is a **new revenue line + a sales door-opener** — the **"Website Service"** is already live (J intapad pays **£5/mo**, Stripe product `prod_UsxCmHbQlA6RrU`).

**Two ways we can look after a client's website:**
- **Path A — manage it where it is.** Client adds us as a Contributor/Editor (Wix, Squarespace, GoDaddy…) or gives us FTP; we do the updates in place. We can still drop our **booking + takeaway widgets** into their existing site (they're just `<script>` embeds).
- **Path B — bring it onto our hosting.** Download their site (or build a new one) → deploy to our Netlify → **repoint their domain** (client always keeps ownership of their own domain — never locked in) → free SSL. This is where we can wire the **live EPOS loop** (menu auto-pulled from their till + online booking + 0%-commission takeaway), the way the Baan Siam demo site does.

**What this means for each of you:**
- **Maya** — new thing to sell: *"we'll host and look after your website too — and it plugs straight into your till: live menu, online booking, and takeaway with zero commission."* Strong upsell for any restaurant that already has a website (or needs one).
- **Nick** — pricing input welcome. Website Service is £5/mo today (host-only). Worth a tier: host-only vs host + EPOS-integrated vs host + ongoing updates.
- **Sandy** — you build & own the client sites. The EPOS-integrated reference is `client/MockUp Website/` (Baan Siam: live `/api/menu` fetch + widget embeds). First one is Chart Thai — see your ticket below.
- **Pose** — billing already covers it (Stripe "Website Service" product; ops reads the catalogue). Website-only clients bill the same way as EPOS clients.
- **Krit owns the hosting mechanics** (deploy pipeline, DNS cutover). Infra tokens (Netlify/Cloudflare/etc.) stay **local** in Control Room — never in the cloud ops app.

**First migration in flight:** Chart Thai — their current site (a generic bought template on the client's FTP host) is now mirrored onto our hosting as a preview, and Sandy's proper Chart Thai build is deployed alongside it for comparison. A cloud/online-sold voucher is re-redeemable every visit. **Y2 = cloud under-reports only, local money SAFE** (the closed-bills feed is `closed_at`-cursor-gated so an open cloud bill isn't re-pulled; the card payment just never reaches the cloud → ops/reports/second-device under-count non-cash). **Y4/Y5 = visible diary/completion revert every ~5s** over the feed's [−7d,+30d] window — operational, not money loss; **ask the client if they've seen diary edits bounce back** (absence would hint the till is usually offline for those). 1. **M2 (HIGH, money)** — `widget.js:542-598`: session-bundle count comes from the client, never checked vs amount paid. Traced end-to-end: pay £69 (Stripe binds `initial_value`) but post `total_sessions:10` → 10 sessions; on redeem (`vouchers.js:339-341`) the per-session `amount_used=£6.90` is cosmetic — each session buys a **full** treatment, and the match is **duration-based** (`vouchers.js:318-338`) so it redeems against any same-duration (even pricier) treatment. Cap is 50 → one £69 payment can mint up to 50 full treatments. Fix = mirror `/book`'s amount-match (`widget.js:305-308`). ⚠️ Live only if the bundle-purchase widget is customer-facing with real Stripe (confirm w/ Korakot — may be demo-only like M1).

3. **R1 (HIGH, reports)** — `reports.js` `<col>::date = $1::date` buckets by UTC on BOTH backends (node-pg sends no session TZ; Railway `TZ` env is on the Node container, not the managed PG). A 00:30 BST bill lands on yesterday's Z. **This is the SEPOS-048 class you just fixed on the restaurant — port the JS UTC-window approach.** Proven in psql.
4. **G1 (HIGH, privacy)** — `widget.js:485`: public `/api/widget/book` echoes an existing client's STORED name → phone/email→name oracle on spa clientele. One line — return the submitted name.

### ✅ HIGHBURY — online booking + deposit fully FIXED & VERIFIED (Krit, 2026-08-01→02, confirmed by Korakot)
Highbury (first own-keys spa taking a real deposit) surfaced a **3-bug chain in the shared spa booking widget/payment path — all now fixed in shared code, so every spa client with a deposit policy is protected:**
1. **payment-intent 500** — `stripeGateway` own-keys mode returned `opts:{}`; stripe-node v16 rejects an empty object as request-options ("Unknown arguments ([object Object])") → deposits couldn't even start (widget showed £0.00). Fix: `opts:undefined`.
2. **hang on "processing card"** — `confirmPayment` had no `return_url`; UK live cards trigger 3-D Secure which needs it, so confirm never resolved (+ no `.catch`, so silent hang). Fix: `confirmParams.return_url` + `.catch`.
3. **"could not retrieve data from the specified Element"** — `confirmPayment` called `render()` (which does `modal.innerHTML=''`) BEFORE confirming, unmounting the Stripe card iframe. Fix: update the pay button in place; only re-render after confirm.
**VERIFIED end-to-end on the live widget** (drove a real booking to the payment step, clicked Pay): card field mounts, no Element error, no hang, Pay reaches Stripe cleanly (empty-card → "card number is incomplete"). Only the final valid-card→3DS→booking-created step is untested (needs a real charge in live mode). Korakot confirmed "it's good now."
Also removed the **destructive rota-seed** (`83ed476` "fill missing days") that re-filled deliberately-off days on every boot — my earlier full-main deploy tripped it and reset Highbury's rota (they re-filled; nothing to undo). **Two lessons logged:** [[feedback_isolate_fix_never_touch_client_data]] (deploy ONLY the isolated fix on the client's running SHA; diff boot migrations/seeds that write client data) + verify on the real surface before handing to Korakot. **Open Highbury item: IONOS cache/login** — widgets are correct but a server-side IONOS cache hides them from logged-out customers (clears on its own / instantly with their IONOS login; same login also enables the new-design domain swap).

### 🚦 GO-LIVE CHECKPOINT — before ANY client (ANY product) takes real payments (Krit, 2026-08-02)
Applies to **restaurant, spa AND shop** — not just spa. Whenever a client goes live taking real money (spa **booking deposits**, restaurant **online takeaway** / **gift vouchers**, shop **checkout**), run a payment go-live check on THAT tenant. The Highbury 3-bug chain got shipped because a payment path went live without an end-to-end verification on the tenant — that's a cross-product risk. Steps (adapt the endpoint per product):
1. **Config:** the tenant's own `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` / `STRIPE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` / `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` set + **all the same mode** (live vs test — a mismatch is the #1 killer); webhook created in that mode with the right events; verify via the tenant's stripe-config endpoint (`configured:true`, right `pk_`).
2. **Code is current on THAT tenant** — the deploy model differs by product, so confirm the fix is actually running: **restaurant** tenants auto-deploy from GitHub `main` (a push hits all restaurant backends — verify via `/api/health` commit); **spa** tenants deploy **individually via `railway up`** (a shared fix reaches a spa tenant only on its own redeploy — `grep` the served `widget.js` for the fix, e.g. `return_url` + `DO NOT render`); **tills/frontends** (desktop tag, Sunmi APK, manual-vs-auto Netlify) are separate channels again — see [[feedback_deploy_applies_to_everyone]].
3. **Server payment endpoint works:** hit the tenant's create-intent/order endpoint (spa `/api/widget/payment-intent`, restaurant `/api/takeaway/payment-intent`, etc.) → returns a `client_secret`/order id, not a Stripe SDK/arg error.
4. **E2E surface check (the step that actually catches it):** drive the real flow to the payment step in a browser; the Stripe card field mounts; empty-card Pay shows a normal validation ("card number is incomplete") — NOT "could not retrieve data" and NOT an endless "processing".
5. **No destructive boot migration:** confirm nothing in the tenant's boot path overwrites configured client data (the spa "fill missing days" rota-seed was the offender — removed 2026-08-01). Diff `<running-sha>..HEAD` for boot INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE on client tables before any deploy.
⚠️ **Jinta still needs a `railway up`** to pick up the spa payment fixes + rota-seed removal (deposit:none today, so unaffected — do it before it ever enables deposits). **Sam owns spa go-lives; Krit/Sandy/Maya/Nook restaurant; Joy shop — everyone runs this before their product's client takes real money.** Full checklist: memory [[reference_golive_payment_checkpoint]].

Fixed: one-line pass-through + prompt now teaches case-of-N×size ("2 cases 6×1L @ £28.50" → pack_size 6, pack_unit L). His 5 scanned ingredients hand-repaired to metric+bridges on local+cloud (Kikkoman ml @£0.00475 w/ case=6000ml, Duck kg w/ each=2.1kg, etc.). **Verify after v1.8.11:** rescan the same PNG — all 5 should fuzzy-MATCH and convert via bridges into ml/kg.

### ✅ SHIPPED — SEPOS-INV-UNITS-001: inventory unit intelligence — v1.8.10 PUBLISHED (Krit, 2026-07-28)
**BUILT + LIVE-TESTED same session.** Confirm now reconciles units in priority order: same-unit (aliased) → pure metric (L↔ml/kg↔g) → per-ingredient purchase bridge (new `ingredients.purchase_unit`+`purchase_to_usage`, '1 each = 2.1 kg') → AI pack info (prompt now extracts pack_size/pack_unit) → else the line is **SKIPPED + reported with a fix-it hint** (`skipped_unit_mismatch`, red panel on the scanner done-screen) — the blind-stamp corruption trap is CLOSED. Pack-smart auto-create: new ingredients land in ml/g with pro-rated cost AND a self-configured bridge. IngredientsTab gains Bought-as + bridge fields. 5-line test matrix on a DB copy: ALL exact (2L@£35/L→£0.035/ml · 3 each ducks→6.3kg@£3.8095/kg · same-unit · bridgeless case refused · 500ml-bottle auto-create). Backend live on all tenants via push; desktop rides v1.8.10. Original gap notes: Two gaps surfaced while speccing Fern's inventory: (1) **no metric conversion** — recipe amounts must be entered in the SAME unit the ingredient is priced in (20 "ml" against a £35/L ingredient silently costs £700, 1000× off); (2) **no each↔metric bridge** — items bought "each" (duck £8) but cooked in grams need manual arithmetic at goods-in. ⚠️ **Plus a real corruption trap found in code:** invoice confirm blindly stamps invoice qty→stock and unit_price→cost_per_unit with NO unit reconciliation — a "2 each @ £8" line matched to a per-kg ingredient corrupts stock AND all recipe costs (server.js ~5848). **Design:** per-ingredient purchase↔usage conversion (pack size "1L bottle"→1000ml; avg weight "1 duck≈2.1kg"), AI scanner extracts pack size, confirm auto-converts and REFUSES unreconcilable lines. Touches cost maths → build + test carefully, not a late-night ship. **Interim staff rule (in force):** ingredient unit = the unit recipes use (price small stuff per ml/g); only match invoice lines whose unit agrees; whole-ish items (eggs/limes/ducks-in-quarters) work well as "each" with fractional recipe amounts.

### 🚨 SEPOS-STATION-004 — THE bug that made station routing "not work" from the till — FOUND VIA LIVE TRACE + FIXED — ✅ v1.8.7 PUBLISHED, fix VERIFIED on the till (Edamame → .100 from a real UI send) (Krit, 2026-07-28)
Korakot kept reporting per-dish/station routing failing from the ORDER SCREEN while every direct API test routed perfectly. Root cause nailed with a print-request trace tap on the running till: **`sendOrder`'s `cartAsItems` projection (predates stations) silently DROPPED `menu_item_id` from every printed line** — the station split looks dishes up by that id, so all UI-printed tickets lost routing identity and fell to the role default. One-line fix (include `menu_item_id`, null-safe for Misc lines); verified on the rig: same send now traces `menu_item_id=216 → station .100, def 0`. Also fixes the identical gap in the Sunmi native split (never noticed — Chart Thai has one printer). **v1.8.7 tagged.** Lesson recorded: UI-path testing ≠ API-path testing — verify on the user's actual surface (the trace tap technique is the tool: append req.body log into the installed bundle's plain-file server.js, restart, reproduce, read).

### 🖨️ SEPOS-STATION-002 — TRUE multi-station printing — ✅ v1.8.4 CONFIRMED WORKING ON THE LIVE TILL (Krit, 2026-07-28)
Korakot wired a **4th printer** for multi-station restaurants. Two gaps closed today:
1. **Per-station dine-in printing on desktop/browser tills** — SEPOS-STATION-001 only routed stations for takeaway + native Sunmi; the dine-in server prints always hit the single role printer. Now `/api/print/kitchen|kitchen-full|bar` split items by `categories.printer_id` (same model as the takeaway router, reuses `printKitchenToPrinter`). **Fail-safe:** a dead station rescues its items onto the main kitchen ticket — food can never be silently lost. No stations assigned = byte-identical behaviour (Thann Thai unaffected). **Live-tested on the Fern 4-printer rig** (dev server on a DB copy): sushi physically printed on the `.103` station, ramen on the kitchen default; dead-station test rescued cleanly, no CUPS diversion.
2. **Un-assigning a category route now clears on desktop** — `upsertRows` gained opt-in null-sync; `categories.printer_id` opted in (the remove-side of the v1.8.3 add fix).
**v1.8.4 PUBLISHED — 8 assets in BOTH repos, manifests verified 1.8.4.** Backend pushed (station code inert on cloud — can't reach LAN printers). **Printer field-day lessons (same day):** POS80 units can ship with DUPLICATE MACs from the factory — two twins fought over the network until one MAC was rewritten via config.cgi (fix + full HTTP/0.9 recipe in Krit's memory `project_pos80_ethernet_printer_gotchas`); Fern rig now 4 printers, 4 unique IPs+MACs, all roles correct (Bills .100 / Kitchen .101 / Bar .102 / Sushi station .103). CONFIRMED on the Fern till: version-probe sushi item printed at its .102 station from the RUNNING till server. Gotcha discovered: a restart seconds after the update downloads can launch the OLD image one last time (Squirrel mid-swap) — if a just-updated till misroutes, quit fully, wait 10s, reopen. Also field-fixed same day: 4th printer moved off wrong subnet (192.168.68.200→.103 via raw HTTP/0.9 config), duplicate factory MACs on two POS80s (one rewritten via config.cgi). NEXT TICKET (Korakot wants it): SEPOS-STATION-003 — per-DISH 'Prints at' override in Edit Item (menu_items.printer_id, inherit-from-category default) for dishes inside one category that belong to different stations.

Config is already multi-tenant-safe (serve-origin `API` + per-script `data-restaurant`) — **only DOM namespacing blocks it**. Options: **A** branch-picker → one widget per page (zero dev, and it matches how Nua's `/reservation` already works) · **B** namespace IDs with an instance counter + drop the 3 guards (contained, one file) · **C** group widget with location as step 0. **Maya recommends A now, B as a ticket if Nua signs.**

- **🎬 HERO VIDEO built from their OWN stills** — Nua has **no video anywhere** (no `<video>`, no YouTube/Vimeo, `wp/v2/media?media_type=video` returns 0), so rather than use stock we rendered a 23s Ken-Burns + crossfade film via ffmpeg (`make-hero-video.sh`, reusable): 1280×720, **2.1MB**, faststart, yuv420p, muted/looping, poster fallback, and **disabled on mobile + prefers-reduced-motion** so it never burns a customer's data. ⚠️ ffmpeg gotcha logged in the script: `zoompan` emits `d` frames PER INPUT FRAME — feeding it a 4s clip produced a 460s/92MB render.
- **⚠️ Local-preview caveat:** `python3 -m http.server` does NOT support HTTP Range, so video playback hangs the page; use the range-capable server in the scratchpad. Chrome-extension CDP also froze twice on video decode, so **live playback is verified only at the file level** (ffprobe + a frame contact-sheet confirm content/encoding are correct) — **eyeball the hero once it's deployed.**
- **⚠️ Branch photo attribution:** Wycombe is definitive (their phone number is on the shopfront sign); Fitzrovia/Reading/Kingston are my best inference from the archive — worth a glance before this goes near the client.
- **🔒 Safety measure I added:** because this mockup takes REAL bookings, a stranger finding it could get a confirmation and believe they hold a table at Nua. So every page is `noindex,nofollow`, `robots.txt` is `Disallow: /`, and each booking page carries a visible notice that it reaches a SiamEPOS demo restaurant with the branch's real phone number for actual bookings.
- **🎨 HERO REDESIGNED (Korakot: "design below your standard" — fair) + redeployed.** The original hero was the default templated split — eyebrow/serif-headline/lede/2-buttons left, framed food photo right, on flat dark — and it buried the hero video at 34% opacity behind that card. Verified desktop + 390px mobile (both strong). Sections below unchanged. Same URL.

CLI was already authenticated as Korakot so no token handling was needed. Verified live: all **9 pages 200**, `robots.txt` = `Disallow: /`, `noindex,nofollow` on every page, video served with **HTTP 206 range support**.
- **⚠️ HERO VIDEO PLAYBACK STILL UNVERIFIED — needs a human eyeball.** On the live site the `<video>` sits at `networkState:2` (LOADING) / `readyState:0` (nothing decoded) indefinitely, and `play()` froze the renderer twice. Evidence says the FILE is fine, not the site: ffprobe reports valid h264/yuv420p/faststart/23.4s, a frame contact-sheet renders correctly, `canPlayType('avc1.4d401f')` returns **"probably"**, and Netlify serves 206. Conclusion: this automated Chrome profile can't decode media. **Graceful degradation is in place either way** — the poster frame paints, so the hero is never blank. **Korakot/anyone: open https://nua-sandy.netlify.app in a normal browser and confirm the hero moves.**

### 📌 SPA-SETTINGS-SYNC — till settings now push to the cloud + stick — ✅ SHIPPED + LIVE-VERIFIED (Krit, 2026-07-09, spa v0.2.39 + cloud)
**Symptom:** Spa name saved on the till reverted to "Highbury" after logout/login; then (v0.2.38) Save kicked the operator to the login screen. **Two root causes, both fixed:**
1. **Cloud mount guard** — `app.use('/api/settings', requireAuth, …)` demanded a staff JWT BEFORE the router's own auth, so the till's `x-sync-secret` push was rejected at the mount; the cloud never got the save and the cloud-wins pull reverted it. Now auth is per-route (EPOS pattern): GET=`requireAuth`, PUT=`settingsAuth` (admin/manager JWT **or** sync-secret).
2. **`requireRole` needs `requireAuth` first** — `requireRole` reads `req.staff`, populated ONLY by `requireAuth`. Calling it alone 401s every VALID token → client treats it as a dead session → login-kick. Fixed by chaining `requireAuth → requireRole` in `settingsAuth`. **Team rule (spa repo): never use `requireRole` without `requireAuth` in front** — audited all other routers, they're safe (mount-level `requireAuth` intact).
- Ported the restaurant's settings-sync to the spa till: `settings.js` enqueues `update_setting` → `syncService.drainSettings()` pushes to cloud each tick BEFORE the pull (independent of the stuck B3 drain, retries offline) → `pullConfig` pending-guard skips keys with un-pushed local changes (no revert window).
- **Live-verified on the cloud (8/8 sweep):** health `build`=deployed sha; sync-secret PUT→200 + persisted + read back via /api/sync/config; forged JWT→"invalid token"; no auth→401; GET gated 401; widget/branding public 200; sync feed gated 401; releases/latest→v0.2.39. `/api/health` now reports the deployed git `build` sha (deploys were never the problem — the mount guard was).
- **v0.2.38 GitHub release marked prerelease + DO-NOT-INSTALL note** (has the login-kick bug); auto-update goes straight to **v0.2.39** (8/8 assets incl. Mac-OTA `.zip`). Highbury's till (v0.2.37) auto-updates on next restart; its old fire-and-forget settings push also works now the cloud accepts it.
- **Still open (unchanged):** general offline push (appointments/bills created offline) = real Phase B3 work.

## ⭐ CLIENT ONBOARDING PROCESS — the canonical go-live flow (updated 2026-06-24)

> **This is how a new restaurant/spa gets from "signed" to LIVE.** All of it is driven from the **back-office → Client Detail → Onboarding tab** (`ops.siamepos.co.uk`). As of 2026-06-24 the final step is a **hard security gate** — a client **cannot** flip to `live` until it passes (SEPOS-SECGATE-001).

**The steps, in order (each ticks its checklist item automatically where it can):**

1. **Add the client** — New Client wizard (name, owner, email, plan, product `restaurant`|`spa`). Generates slug + `SYNC_SECRET`.
2. **Railway backend** — "🚂 Open in Railway" deep-links the template with `SYNC_SECRET`/`BREVO`/`ANTHROPIC`/identity pre-filled. Deploy, then paste the **service URL + DATABASE_URL** into **🔐 Setup → Tenant infrastructure**. ⚠️ Each service needs its **own unique `AUTH_SECRET`/`JWT_SECRET`** (see gate, step 8).
3. **Netlify frontend** — "🚀 Provision Netlify now" creates the site, links the repo, sets `VITE_API_URL`, attaches the `<slug>.siamepos.co.uk` subdomain.
4. **Seed the DB** — "🚀 Run seed SQL now" loads staff + settings into the tenant Postgres (or download the SQL to run by hand).
5. **Import the menu** — AI scanner or manual.
6. **Desktop + SYNC_SECRET** — send the owner the installer + their `SYNC_SECRET` (surfaced on this tab).
7. **Owner remote login** — "🔑 Provision owner login" creates the owner's **email + password** sign-in on the tenant (POSTs the tenant's secret-gated `/api/auth/set-credentials`) and returns a one-time temp password to relay. *(Requires step 8's secret already set.)*
8. **🔒 GO-LIVE SECURITY GATE (blocks `live`)** — three checks, all must pass:
   - **Tenant secret set** — *verified live*: the back-office probes the tenant with the public default secret and only passes on `403`. **Can't be ticked by hand.** → set a unique `AUTH_SECRET` (restaurant) / `JWT_SECRET` (spa) on the tenant Railway first.
   - **Default `0000` admin PIN changed** — operator attests.
   - **Temp provisioning / Railway CLI tokens revoked** — operator attests.

   Only when **all onboarding steps AND the gate** pass does the client auto-flip **setup → live** and stamp `go_live_date`. If every step is ticked but the gate is unmet, the tab shows a red *"security gate blocking"* banner.

**Why the gate exists:** secret/PIN/token hardening used to live as deferred to-dos and got skipped until something bit (the spa shipped with no `JWT_SECRET` = live forgeable-admin hole). It's now enforced, not remembered.

**Pose** — this lives in your repo. Endpoints: `GET/PUT /api/clients/:id/security`, `POST /api/clients/:id/security/verify`; gate state in `metadata.security`; one `goLiveReady()` helper guards every status-flip path. Provisioning/owner-login/gate all need `metadata.tenant_railway_url` set.

## 🛒 SiamShop — New Product

SiamShop is a **separate standalone product** for Thai supermarkets wanting to sell online.
NOT part of SiamEPOS. Own repo at `~/Desktop/siamshop/`. Agent: **Joy**.
Tickets prefixed **SIAMSHOP-***. Stack: React+Vite (Netlify) · Node+Express (Railway) · PostgreSQL · Stripe · Brevo.
First prospect already interested — need SIAMSHOP-001→003 for a working demo.
Launch Joy: `cd ~/Desktop/siamshop && claude --model claude-fable-5`
📓 **Joy's dev notebook: `~/Desktop/siamshop/Joy.md`** (next to `CLAUDE.md`) — read it with CLAUDE.md at session start: real architecture (one same-origin Railway service, **not** Netlify), the 3-shop fork model + how to port features, Railway/Postgres outage gotchas, feature patterns, and how to work with Korakot.

### 🤖 FEATURE: Facebook Messenger AI order-taking (LIVE) — team explainer

**This is SiamShop's headline differentiator. Maya/Sandy/Nick — use this in every pitch.**

**The problem it kills:** Thai supermarkets already take loads of orders by Facebook
Messenger (customers DM a list of what they want). Today the owner reads each message
and **types the bill by hand** — slow, error-prone, and it doesn't update stock or records.

**What SiamShop does instead — fully automatic, no staff:**
1. Customer messages the shop's Facebook Page a normal list, e.g. *"2x jasmine rice 5kg, 1 coconut milk, น้ำปลา 1 ขวด"* (Thai or English).
2. **AI (Claude) reads the message**, matches each item to the shop's real product catalogue, and ignores anything it can't find.
3. It **prices everything from live stock/prices** and replies in seconds with a tidy summary + subtotal + a **ready-to-pay checkout link** (and flags anything not found / under the min order).
4. Customer taps the link → pays by **card (Stripe) or bank transfer** and enters their delivery address.
5. The order lands in the shop's admin **tagged `source = messenger`**, decrements the **same shared stock** as the till + website, and triggers the confirmation emails.

**Why it sells:** "Your customers order the way they already do — in Messenger — but you
never type a bill again. The AI does it, takes the money, and updates your stock." It meets
Thai shoppers where they are (Facebook-first) and removes the owner's biggest daily chore.
Works bilingually (Thai/English). One stock core means Messenger + walk-in till + website
all show true stock and combined "what sold".

**Status:** built + LIVE end-to-end (ticket SIAMSHOP-011). Bot replies on the connected
Page; checkout links point at the live site. ⚠️ While the FB app is in **Development mode**
it only replies to app admins/testers — going public needs Meta **App Review**
(`pages_messaging` permission), a step to schedule before a client's public launch.

**Tech note (for devs):** webhook `POST /api/messenger/webhook` (signature-verified via
`MESSENGER_APP_SECRET`); parsing in `src/services/aiService.js` (Claude `claude-opus-4-8`,
json_schema) + `messengerService.js` (Graph API send). Env on Railway: `MESSENGER_VERIFY_TOKEN`,
`MESSENGER_APP_SECRET`, `MESSENGER_PAGE_ACCESS_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`. Logs `[messenger]` lines per step.

| 2026-07-08 | Krit | **🔴 Self-healing sync queue — a poison action can no longer freeze a till's sync (SEPOS-SYNC-HEAL-001) → desktop v1.6.121.** Live-support: Korakot's Baan Siam **desktop till** had "sync queue backing up — 13 actions" stuck for ~1.5 days AND a **phantom table 1** (occupied 36h on the till, absent on the webapp). **Root cause:** `syncService.syncOnce()` stopped on the FIRST failing action and `return`ed — so item #191 (`pay_order` for order 50, a bill **already closed on cloud** with cloud_id 189 → re-pay 4xx's forever) **permanently blocked every action behind it**, including `create_order` for table 1's live order #54 (never reached cloud → phantom on the floor, £78.90, 6 items never fired). Head-of-line block. **Fix:** the drain loop now **classifies failures** — 4xx (already-closed/not-found/conflict) = permanent → **quarantine that item (synced=2) and CONTINUE draining** (live orders behind it sync); 401/403 = auth → stop + surface (check SYNC_SECRET); 5xx = retry, capped at 6 then quarantine; network/timeout = outage → defer + retry, no attempt burn. `sync_queue` gains `attempts`/`last_error`/`failed_at` (PG+SQLite+migration). Quarantined items **surface**: "⚠ Failed to sync" section in the Sync-queue modal (reason + Dismiss), a ⚠ navbar pill, and `failed_actions` in `/api/sync/health` — so a payment that genuinely didn't reach cloud is **visible, not silently lost**. **A remote client's till now self-heals with zero intervention** (the old "Skip" trick needed someone at the device — and Skip only stopped syncing, it did NOT close the local order, which is why "table 1 kept its order"). Local-mode only; cloud web POS untouched. Verified: classification logic 9/9, all files compile, client builds. **Shipped:** pushed (backend auto-deploys all tenants, inert on cloud) + **tag v1.6.121** (CI building DMG/EXE/auto-update); sw v86→**v87**; **folded into Fri APK v1.4.32** (memory `project_friday_kick_sunmi_apk` — matters just as much on the Sunmi host). **Also:** cleared Baan Siam's phantom **table 1** — local-only cancel of order 54 (NOT via the app's cancel flow, which would've posted a cancel to an unrelated *cloud* order #54 since 54 had no cloud_id); queue left clean. | SEPOS-SYNC-HEAL-001 · desktop v1.6.121 |

| 2026-06-23 | Krit | **SEPOS-PRO-008 — accurate per-customer SPEND tracking (orders.reservation_id), shipped v1.6.101.** Customer CRM spend was a **heuristic** — it summed closed bills matching the booking's `table_id` + date, so a table with two bookings in a day **shared/double-counted** the spend, and walk-ins were blind. Now bills link **exactly** to their booking: **(1)** new `orders.reservation_id` column — PG `ALTER … ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` (`ON DELETE SET NULL` so deleting a booking NEVER deletes the bill) + SQLite CREATE + `addColumnIfMissing`. **(2)** when a bill is paid on a **seated** table, `orders.reservation_id` is stamped to that booking (reuses the existing SEPOS-044 seated-reservation lookup at close, so no new flow). **(3)** the two CRM aggregation queries (`fetchCustomersForSegment` + `/api/customers`) now attribute spend **exactly via reservation_id**, keeping the old table+date join ONLY as a fallback for unlinked/legacy bills — `o.reservation_id = r.id OR (o.reservation_id IS NULL AND table+date)` so linked bills count once (no double-count) and history still shows numbers. Takeaway spend was already exact (customer is on the order row). **Deploy:** backend on `git push` (migration runs on boot, idempotent); **desktop tills get it via v1.6.101 auto-update** (Baan Siam is desktop-only — CRM viewed against local SQLite, so it needed the release). **Still heuristic (by design, flagged):** walk-ins with no booking aren't attributed to a customer (no customer captured at the till — a future "attach customer at till" ticket would close that); legacy bills before this change stay on the fallback. CSV header still says "(est.)" since spend is now a *mix* of exact + estimated. | SEPOS-PRO-008 → v1.6.101 |

| 2026-06-11 | Krit (Cowork) | **v1.6.67 — Till sessions / overnight shifts (EposNow-style Open Shift → Close Shift) (SEPOS-053, tag `v1.6.67`). SHIPPED — Korakot smoke-tested on the till; pushed + tagged; backend deploy verified live (`GET /api/till-sessions/current` → 200 `{session:null}`, so the `till_sessions` migration ran on boot).** Korakot asked why we can't leave a shift open overnight (even two nights) then close it, like EposNow. Root reason: our Z-report totals by **calendar date** (`closed_at::date` window), which is why SEPOS-047l's UTC flip created a midnight-edge risk. EposNow totals by **trading session**: open a shift → every sale stamps that session → Close Shift (Z) sums `WHERE session = N`, so a shift spans midnight/two nights with **zero timezone dependence**. We had no session object, so we couldn't. **Built (7 files, +276):** new `till_sessions` table (PG + SQLite) with a **partial unique index = one open shift per restaurant**; `orders.session_id` stamped at every close/cancel via one shared correlated subquery; `GET/POST /api/till-sessions/current|open|close` (409 if already open; desktop write-through to cloud reusing the SEPOS-047g forwarder); **Z-report preview accepts `session_id`** and scopes by the shift's own open→close **instants** (not `::date`) so it's midnight-safe; `till_sessions` added to PULL_TABLES + instant pull-back; Z Report screen gets an Open/Close-Shift banner (float input, live "N orders · £X" tally). Date-range + End-of-Day Z modes untouched. **Design choice:** reporting keys on the **time window**, not `orders.session_id`, so multi-terminal cloud/local id differences can't corrupt totals — `session_id` is an audit stamp only. **Verified:** backend `node --check` clean; client `vite build` green; new SQL run against a real SQLite engine 7/7 (one-open enforced, multi-tenant isolation, stamp picks the right shift, NULL when no shift, re-open after close, window tally). PWA CACHE_NAME v34→v35. **Caveats:** opened_by/closed_by stored NULL (no logged-in-staff client store found — easy follow-up); cross-terminal `session_id` binding deferred (reporting unaffected by design). **Renumbered 049→053 mid-build** — 049/050/051/052 were taken by other sessions (049 = Reservations Timeline). Spec: `~/Documents/Claude/Projects/SiamEpos/SEPOS-053-Till-Sessions-Overnight-Shifts-Krit-Ticket.md`. Spawned **SEPOS-054** (make the remaining date-range reports timezone-aware via SEPOS-048 `minutesInZone` — lower-stakes now the Z is session-based). **Nook:** open a shift, take an order across midnight (BST), close → all in one Z; confirm a 2nd open is blocked; confirm date-range Z still works; installers (Mac DMG + Win EXE) for v1.6.67 build via the tag — auto-update will pull them. | RELEASE-v1.6.67, SEPOS-053 |
| 2026-06-11 | Krit (Cowork) | **v1.6.66 — FIX the BST +1h timestamp bug (table timer stuck at "0m") (SEPOS-047l, tag `v1.6.66`).** Korakot: a just-opened table showed "0m" and didn't tick up. Diagnosed live on his Baan Siam till: table 2's order had `opened_at=13:51:02Z` while real UTC was `13:20Z` — **31 min in the FUTURE**, so the floor-map timer's `(now - opened)` went negative and clamped to "0m" until real time caught up (~1h), then counted from zero. **Root cause (cloud-wide, every BST timestamp):** `src/db/database.js` ran `SET timezone='Europe/London'` on every PG connection. Since `opened_at`/`closed_at`/`fired_at`… are tz-NAIVE `TIMESTAMP` columns, NOW() wrote BST wall-clock ("13:51"), but the Node process (UTC) read it back as if UTC → every served timestamp was +1h during BST. Confirmed the cloud itself stored the future value (not a client bug). **Fix:** `SET timezone='UTC'` on the connection — tz-naive columns now round-trip correctly (PG writes UTC wall-clock, Node reads UTC). Safe because SEPOS-048 already moved the reservation/takeaway HH:MM validators to `minutesInZone()` using each restaurant's own IANA timezone, so UK time-of-day logic no longer depends on the connection TZ (verified: no remaining CURRENT_TIME/LOCALTIME/::time wall-clock SQL). Fixes the floor-map timer AND every other +1h-off timestamp (kitchen timers, report boundaries, etc.) for NEW rows. **Note:** rows written before this deploy stay 1h off until they close — only live/open timers matter, and the stale open orders were cleaned up (see v1.6.65 row). Server-only — no client/UI. **✅ CONFIRMED LIVE by Korakot:** a freshly-opened table (Table 4) ticks correctly (0m→1m→…); a table opened pre-fix still shows 0m as expected. **Nook:** open a fresh table and confirm the timer starts at 0m and ticks up minute-by-minute (not stuck). | RELEASE-v1.6.66, SEPOS-047l |

Now reads Y/M/D components and adds a day in pure UTC (`Date.UTC`). Verified both paths: SQLite string + PG-BST Date → +1 day correctly. **(LOW, money) Receipt fixed-discount on merged lines:** buildReceipt merges identical rows and recomputed a 'fixed' discount from the MERGED quantity (deducting it once), so two rows each with £5 off printed £19 while the bill charged £14. Verified: merged line nets £14.00. **(LOW, security) HTML injection in booking emails:** customer_name/notes (from the public widget) interpolated raw into confirmation + reminder HTML; added `escapeHtml` at assignment. **All E2E/unit-verified; node --check clean.** Server-only — no PWA bump. **Nook:** VAT report on a bill with a bill-level discount; batch extend on cloud during BST; a receipt with the same discounted dish added as two separate lines. **🎉 Bug hunt complete — 29/29 fixed across v1.6.56–64** (4 internet-exposed security, 6 money/data-loss, 4 desktop SQLite crashes, 3 sync races, 3 frontend, + staff-sync gap + 5 mop-up). | 2026-06-11 | Krit (Cowork) | **v1.6.59 — desktop SQLite dialect batch: 4 PG-only `::int`/ILIKE bugs (SEPOS-047e, tag `v1.6.59`).** From the bug hunt — all FOUR were `::int` casts or ILIKE that are PG-only and a hard SQLite SYNTAX ERROR, so they 500'd ONLY on desktop installs (invisible to cloud testing): (1) **every staff edit** `PUT /api/staff/:id` (`COALESCE($N::int,…)`) — name/PIN/role/active-toggle all failed; (2) **voucher sold/redeemed figures in Reports** (`COUNT(*)::int`) — wrapped in `.catch(()=>0)` so they showed **£0 every day with no error**; (3) **voucher search** `GET /api/vouchers?q=` (ILIKE) — 500'd the instant the operator typed; (4) **batch-recipe delete** (`COUNT(*)::int` in-use guard) — every delete 500'd. **Root cause + fix:** ONE central change to `preTranslate()` in `src/db/localDatabase.js` — added `expr::int → CAST(expr AS INTEGER)` (operand = placeholder `$N`, function call `COUNT(*)`/`SUM(…)`, or column; negative lookahead leaves `::int[]` for the existing ANY-array expansion in translateParams) and `ILIKE → LIKE` (SQLite LIKE is already case-insensitive for ASCII). Fixes these four AND any future `::int`. **E2E-verified on a live DB_MODE=local boot:** staff edit 200 (was 500) + row updated; sold a £40 voucher + redeemed £15 → Reports shows sold {count1,£40}/redeemed {count1,£15} (was £0); voucher search by partial code returns the match; batch-recipe delete 200 (was 500); `ANY($1::int[])` takeaway still works (201); zero SQLite syntax errors in the log. **Swept for other untranslated PG-only constructs on the desktop path — none:** FILTER(WHERE) is supported by the bundled SQLite 3.53.0, the only live `INTERVAL` is the 30-day cleanup which is `DB_MODE!=='local'`-gated (cloud-only), and there's no DISTINCT ON / EXTRACT / DATE_TRUNC / json-ops / other casts. Server-only change — no client/UI, no PWA bump. **Nook:** on a desktop install, regression-test Staff edits, the Reports voucher tiles, voucher search, and batch-recipe delete. ~15 bug-hunt findings remain (sync-races + remaining frontend optimistic-UI). | RELEASE-v1.6.59, SEPOS-047e |

Now forwards quantity+void_type; the void endpoint returns `ghost_item_id` and the sync binds the local partial-void ghost to the cloud ghost (`ghostLocalId`) so the next pullActiveOrders UPDATEs it instead of INSERTing a 2nd ghost (would've double-counted locally). **(4) delete_order could delete the WRONG cloud order:** server enqueued `cloudOrderId: order.cloud_id || orderId` — the raw local SQLite PK when cloud_id was unbound (offline-created or deleted within the ~5s create-push window) → cloud cascade-deleted whatever order owned that id. | 2026-06-11 | Krit (Cowork) | **v1.6.54 — kitchen/bar ticket print latency fix (tag `v1.6.54`).** Korakot asked why tickets take a while to come out after Send Order. Diagnosis: NOT WiFi — the delay is in `printService.js`. (1) Every print deliberately waits ~2.3s (0.6-0.8s drain + fixed 1.5s WAVLINK buffer-flush wait) — **left untouched**, it prevents garbled back-to-back tickets. (2) Copies multiply it; kitchen + bar tickets serialise through one queue. (3) **The fixable one:** on LPR-only print servers (older WAVLINK/TP-Link) every ticket first tried RAW 9100 and burned up to 6s timeout + 1.5s error-wait before falling back to LPR — on EVERY job. **Fixed:** per-IP transport cache (once LPR succeeds, later tickets skip RAW; self-heals by re-probing the full RAW→LPR→CUPS chain if the cached route fails), and RAW connect timeout 6s→2s (LAN printers answer in ms). Worst case drops ~7.5s → 0s on the cached path. Verified with a fake local 9100 printer (happy path ~2.3s unchanged) + refused-port fallback chain. Server-only change, no UI, no PWA bump. **Nook:** worth a print regression pass on the real WAVLINK when convenient — first ticket after app restart still probes RAW once (expected), second ticket should fire noticeably faster. | RELEASE-v1.6.54, PRINT-LATENCY |

Bills footer-total grid template fixed to match the new 7-col layout (the v1.6.14 header change had left it on the old 8-col template). **[v1.6.16]** Three follow-ups: (1) **Z Report device-time fix** — From/To defaults were 1 hour behind during BST because we sliced `toISOString()` (UTC) into `<input type="datetime-local">` which the browser parses as LOCAL. Reformatted defaults from `getHours()` / `getMinutes()` directly so the boxes match the device wall clock; server-side `from` / `to` still go through `toISOString()` in `loadReport()` so UTC comparison vs `orders.closed_at` stays correct. (2) Trading summary's Recent Orders row dropped `· #{order.id}` (consistent with Bills + Reports — operators reference by Table + time) + switched row total to `paid_amount`. (3) **Food vs Drink split** — replaced the "Subtotal (food + drink)" line on Reports + Z Report with 🍽️ Food + 🍺 Drink rows (split via `categories.is_bar` at item level, per-item discounts applied via `SUM(CASE…)` group-by). VAT report extended to surface `is_bar` per line; new `by_kind` block in `/api/reports/vat` splits net/VAT/gross/items between food (is_bar=0) and drink (is_bar=1); UI renders a two-up Food/Drink summary card under the per-rate breakdown; CSV carries the by_kind rows too. Footer text clarified that per-item discounts ARE applied; only bill-level discount + the optional 12.5% service charge sit outside the report. **[v1.6.17] Mac SQLite regression hotfix** — v1.6.16's new food/drink query used PG's `GREATEST(0, …)` inside `SUM(CASE …)`. `dbAdapter` handles `::date` / `::timestamp` / `NOW()` / `ANY($N::int[])` / `FOR UPDATE` / `TO_CHAR` but NOT `GREATEST`, so better-sqlite3 threw an "unknown function" error, the `Promise.all` rejected, and the whole endpoint returned 500 — blanking Trading + Reports + Z Report on every Mac install. Cloud (PG) installs were fine. Fix: extended `translateSql()` in `src/db/localDatabase.js` with `GREATEST → max` and `LEAST → min` (SQLite's `max()` / `min()` act as scalar comparators when given >1 argument so semantics line up exactly). Future server.js SQL can keep using PG-native forms. **Memory saved**: `feedback_dbadapter_sql_check` so future Krit vets new SQL against the translator's coverage before shipping. **PWA cache bumped v21 → v25** across the four releases. (b) **Reports / Z / Bills service-charge maths** — pick a closed dine-in order with service enabled, verify the headline cards sum `paid_amount` not `orders.total`, Subtotal + Service charge + Total stack matches `subtotal + (subtotal × 12.5%) = paid_amount` to the penny. (c) **VAT report food/drink** — verify the two-up card numbers tie out to per-rate totals + that drink rows are everything with `categories.is_bar=1`. (d) **Z Report time inputs** — open during BST, confirm From/To match the device wall clock, then save a shift Z-Report and verify the saved row's `total_sales` matches `total_cash + total_card + total_other`. (e) **Mac local install** — DB_MODE=local on a v1.6.17 install, confirm Trading / Reports / Z Report show real numbers (would be blank on v1.6.16). | RELEASE-v1.6.14, RELEASE-v1.6.15, RELEASE-v1.6.16, RELEASE-v1.6.17, SEPOS-PAY-AMEND-001 |

| 2026-05-29 | Krit | **SEPOS-040 — real Stripe payment + Apple Pay + Google Pay on takeaway widget (commit `7ece13a`).** Nook's earlier QA pass (17/18) caught that Sandy's scaffold was missing the actual backend endpoints — `payment-intent` 503'd because it didn't exist, `stripe-config` returned defaults, no webhook handler for `payment_intent.succeeded`. This commit fills all 4 gaps. **Backend (`src/server.js`):** new `GET /api/takeaway/stripe-config` (returns `configured` boolean + publishable key for widget to detect Stripe is on); new `POST /api/takeaway/payment-intent` (creates Stripe PI with `automatic_payment_methods: { enabled: true }` — THAT flag is what makes Apple Pay + Google Pay + Link appear in PaymentElement; £500 sanity cap defends against client-side amount tampering); new `GET /.well-known/apple-developer-merchantid-domain-association` serves the per-restaurant Stripe domain-verification file from a `STRIPE_APPLE_PAY_DOMAIN_FILE` env var (without this, Apple Pay button never appears on iOS Safari even with the device supporting it — most-missed step in Stripe Apple Pay setups); webhook `payment_intent.succeeded` handler — looks for `metadata.product = 'siamepos_takeaway'` and flips `orders.payment_status = 'paid'`, emits `takeaway_status` socket so kitchen view updates without polling. **Widget (`public/takeaway-widget.js`):** Sandy's `CardElement` (card-only) swapped for Stripe's modern `PaymentElement` (unified UI — auto-shows Apple Pay on iOS Safari, Google Pay on Android Chrome, Link on supported devices, card form fallback elsewhere). Initialised in deferred-intent mode (`mode + amount + currency`, no `clientSecret` yet — PI created server-side at confirm time), so the element rebuild when cart amount changes between Step 3 and Step 4 (otherwise the Apple Pay sheet would charge the old amount — silent UX bug if missed). `elements.submit()` validates BEFORE creating the PI to save a wasted Stripe API call on missing fields. `confirmCardPayment` → `confirmPayment` with `redirect: 'if_required'`. Brand-gold appearance theme (#C9A84C primary). **Architecture decision baked in:** each restaurant uses THEIR OWN Stripe account (per-tenant `pk_live`/`sk_live` in their Railway env), money goes directly to their bank, SiamEPOS Ltd never holds funds. Avoids payment-processing licensing burden. **Backwards-compatible — no-op without Stripe keys.** Restaurants without `STRIPE_*` env vars continue to show the existing demo mock-pay banner; Baan Siam keeps demo mode until you add their keys. **Handoff to Nook (QA round 2):** the test plan you wrote in the Handoffs section is now the right shape. Required for the new tests: set `STRIPE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` + `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` + `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` (test mode) on the production-test Railway → register a webhook in the Stripe dashboard pointing at `/api/stripe/webhook` → run through cards `4242 4242 4242 4242` (success) + `4000 0000 0000 9995` (declined) + verify `payment_status` flips to `paid` on the order row + verify Z-Report splits Stripe takeaway revenue from cash + verify webhook replay safety (Stripe's `idempotency` field — webhook handler uses `payment_status != 'paid'` guard so dupes are no-ops). **Apple Pay device test (separate path, requires Stripe domain verification step done first):** open the widget on iOS Safari with a card saved in Wallet → confirm Apple Pay button appears alongside card form → tap → Face ID/Touch ID prompt → payment succeeds → `orders.payment_status = 'paid'` via webhook. **Defer to v2:** Klarna / SEPA / Bancontact (already auto-supported by `automatic_payment_methods`, just need restaurant to enable in their Stripe dashboard). | SEPOS-040 |

| 2026-05-28 | Krit | **Electron v1.6.3 — printer hardening for client onboarding (tag `v1.6.3`, commit `a9f32f9`).** Full evening of live printer testing on Korakot's Baan Siam install (cnfujun POS80 + WAVLINK then direct-Ethernet) turned into a sweep of receipt + kitchen-ticket fixes plus three robustness passes designed to make a brand-new client's first-day setup self-heal instead of replaying any of tonight's pain. **Receipt fixes:** item names (`undefined` → JOIN menu_items + frontend fallback chain), £ symbol (CP858 codepage init + remap to byte `0x9C`, latin1 encoding so 1 codepoint = 1 byte), table number (LEFT JOIN tables on all 5 print endpoints), whole-receipt centered on paper (was left-aligned with right margin), items grouped on receipt (3x Pad Thai instead of 3 separate `1x Pad Thai` lines — grouped by `menu_item_id + discount + unit_price + course`), notes hidden on receipt (kitchen ticket still has them where chef needs them), course headers `STARTERS`/`MAINS` removed from receipt, `LINE_WIDTH=42` (matches cnfujun POS80, configurable per-install via `settings.printer_line_width` 24–80), logo printing (centered padding to 384 dots + single-command `GS v 0` emit after chunked emit caused PARTIAL prints on cnfujun firmware). **Kitchen ticket fix:** Send Order on an existing table now prints only items just added (`cartAsItems`) — previously `[...existingItems, ...cartAsItems]` re-printed already-cooking dishes every time. **Three hardening passes for client-onboarding stability:** (1) **Bulletproof client-side logo conversion** — `await img.decode()` instead of `img.onload` (large data URLs decode reliably across browsers); threshold auto-escalation 180 → 200 → 220 → 240 (first one with ≥100 inked dots wins, so pastel brand logos still produce visible bitmaps); ink% validation with operator-facing alert if < 100 inked dots; preview thumbnail in Settings showing the exact 1-bit bitmap that'll print + advice text. (2) **CUPS queue auto-detect** — new `GET /api/print/cups-queue-for-ip?ip=192.168.x.x` endpoint shells out to `lpstat -v`; Settings page calls this on IP-blur and auto-fills the CUPS name field; means clients never need to know macOS's auto-naming convention (`_192_168_68_57` for printer at .57). (3) **Realistic mock receipt as Test button** — previously printed a tiny "Printer test OK" page; now prints a full mock receipt (logo + restaurant header + table 5 + 2 covers + 3 mock items + £24.90 subtotal + 12.5% service + £28.01 total) so operators discover layout/£/codepage bugs at setup, not at first service. **Hot-patched into Korakot's v1.6.2 install throughout the evening** then bundled into v1.6.3 once everything verified. **Auto-update**: existing v1.6.x installs pull v1.6.3 silently on next restart; new installs download `SiamEPOS-1.6.3-Setup.dmg` (version in filename — fix from v1.6.2). **Required action for restaurants with logos already uploaded:** open Admin → Settings → 🌐 Network Printers → re-click the logo size button once (S/M/L/Full). This triggers the new bulletproof conversion + saves a centered bitmap. (Or just re-upload the logo file.) Full operator briefing in `RELEASE_NOTES_v1.6.3.md`. **Handoff to Nook:** QA the full receipt path (logo, header, £, table#, item grouping, no notes, no course headers, centered) + kitchen-ticket delta (only new items print after adding to existing order) + the test button shows the mock receipt + CUPS queue auto-fills when typing an IP. | RELEASE-v1.6.3, PRINT-* |
